Arriving for duty! 🫡 pic.twitter.com/M2XEkLC18n

Your Chelsea side today! 🔵 pic.twitter.com/0OvvGVmgpc

A mixture of players who may well be offloaded before the end of the transfer window, those needing to prove themselves, and youngsters you may not have heard of.

Johor Darul Ta’Zim – more commonly known as JDT – are the Man Utd of Malaysia (at least that’s according to a Malaysian friend of mine). In 2013, they were going nowhere and were the dictionary definition of a stagnant club, low on confidence and trophies. That was until the Crown Prince bought the club and, over the next 13 years, transformed it into the powerhouse it is today.

As if to underline JDT’s dominance over Malaysian football they are one game away from the world record unbeaten run in a top flight. The new season of the Malaysian Super League gets under way later this month and should they avoid defeat in their opening match they will extend their unbeaten run - which began in April 2021 - to 109 games and surpass the world record set by Ivorian side Asec Mimosas in the early 1990s.

Chelsea’s pre-season tour of South East Asia ends today with a match against Johor Darul Ta’zim in Malaysia. The clash comes barely 24 hours after the west Londoners beat AC Milan 3-0 in Jakarta, a much-needed win coming on the back of defeats against Juventus and Tottenham.

A fine volley from Moises Caicedo and a double from João Pedro either side of half-time led Xabi Alonso’s side to victory in the Indonesian capital.

The game was fairly even in the first half and looked set to head into the break level at 0-0 but Pedro’s header from Caicedo’s corner put Chelsea in front.

Alonso’s side then doubled their advantage seconds into the second half as Pedro scored his second, assisted by Pedro Neto.

Just minutes later, Chelsea had their third courtesy of a well-worked corner routine. Romeo Lavia’s delivery was met by Caicedo on the volley, with the Ecuadorian’s finish with the outside of the boot finding the bottom corner.

Today, the west London side face Malaysian powerhouses Johor at the Sultan Ibrahim Stadium, more on them later, but considering the tight turnaround from yesterday’s match today’s clash will provide a stern test.

“The idea, with the game against Milan and then one in Johor, was to arrive at this weekend with players not being ready for 90 minutes until now,” Alonso explained after the win in Jakarta.

“We wanted to have a logical progression in their loads; so we had our plan and we’ve stuck to that.

“We have another idea [against Johor]; that was decided yesterday [Friday]. The idea is that many young players were coming to have chances to play tomorrow. If not, we’d have been short, as playing two games in two days is quite intense. So we will need them.”

#2191. Sheffield Wednesday vs Plymouth Argyle predictions with opinion split over Dave Challinor’s first League One game

Sheffield Wednesday face Plymouth Argyle in Dave Challinor‘s first league match in charge of the club. The SWFC boss is tasked with righting the ship, with a four-game winless run in the league leaving them 9th in the table and six points off the automatic promotion spots.

The Owls were beaten in the FA Cup by Oldham Athletic in Challinor’s first game, before a youthful side lost 4-0 to Accrington Stanley in the EFL Trophy on Wednesday night. The new boss will be determined to get off the mark in an important game in the third tier.

As for Plymouth Argyle, they are three points better off than the Owls having played one more game. They sit up in 5th as it stands, winning seven of their 14 League One matches to date.

Unlike their upcoming opponents, the Pilgrims were victorious in their cup matches with wins over Alvechurch and Swindon Town in the FA Cup and EFL Trophy respectively. In their last league game, Tom Cleverley‘s men played out a 0-0 draw against Reading.

“Dave Challinor has led two Sheffield Wednesday games so far, but this feels like the first one that really means something. Points are up for grabs in the bid to climb back up the League One table and after poor results in the cups, he will be determined to get a tune out of his side and put a first win on the board.

“The SWFC boss played a side strong enough to beat Oldham Athletic and he was not impressed as they came up short. It means you have to anticipate changes for a tricky game against Plymouth Argyle.

“The Pilgrims are a good side, but they’ve got some notable absentees through injury. Aribim Pepple and Alex Mitchell are major doubts for the trip to Hillsborough and they will be big misses against an Owls team hungry for all three points.

“Argyle’s away record is strong though, with four wins in the last five League One games on the road. However, I do think their injuries will weaken them here, so I lean towards a valuable win for Challinor and Wednesday.”

“Dave Challinor needs to get off to a winning start with Sheffield Wednesday at Hillsborough this weekend. If SWFC lose, he will be on the back foot already after back-to-back cup losses to Oldham Athletic and Accrington Stanley. In fairness, he played a weakened team against the latter.

“The Owls’ recent form has been dire and their new head coach has a big job on his hands as he looks to get them on track. They have won only one in their last eight matches in all competitions.

“Plymouth Argyle should fancy their chances of getting something in South Yorkshire. PAFC are unbeaten in their last four and will see their upcoming opponents as a promotion rival.

“It is important that the Pilgrims don’t lose on Saturday and they need to be solid at the back. I think Tom Cleverley’s side will have enough to bag themselves a point.”

#2188. West Ham tracking Sheffield United star alongside six Premier League clubs

Sheffield United midfielder Sydie Peck has been among the standout performers for Chris Wilder’s side this term. The SUFC ace is no stranger to transfer interest, and his displays mean rival clubs are regularly keeping tabs on his situation.

A new report from TEAMtalk states Championship rivals and summer admirers West Ham are among those monitoring Peck ahead of a potential January swoop. Also interested in the 22-year-old are six Premier League clubs: Newcastle United, Sunderland, Brighton & Hove Albion, Crystal Palace, Brentford and Bournemouth.

It is added that the Blades are ‘aware’ of the interest in Peck and hope to keep him, but there is an ‘increasing expectation’ that exit opportunities arise in January. The England youth international is currently under contract until 2028, and separate reports have said that a new deal is being eyed.

Peck’s impressive displays have seen his stock surge. He’s a regular in the England U21s setup and his hat-trick against Preston North End showed he has potential to add goals to his game from midfield too.

It means that belief in his ability to step up to the Premier League is growing, and Sheffield United’s uphill battle to break into the Championship promotion fight may well speed up the process. However, Peck himself is in no rush just yet.

Asked about his future after summer transfer speculation, the SUFC star expressed a desire to repay the faith shown in him by Wilder and the first-team and academy coaching staff at Bramall Lane. Speaking to The Star in August, Peck said:

“I joined when I was 16 and the gaffer’s given me every debut since. From Premier League to Championship and in the cup. The staff as well, Mickey Collins. I’ve got a lot of stuff to pay back. They’ve helped me become the player I am today.”

Those comments certainly don’t show a burning desire to move on to a higher level. His loyalty would indicate he has little intention of leaving as early as January too, despite his progress and the growing interest.

Amid this new report and fresh claims from TEAMtalk, Peck’s own words would suggest he is unlikely to exit as early as January.

Peck’s comments aren’t the only reason West Ham could have a tough time persuading him to join. The Hammers spent big on a new midfielder in Arne Engels in the summer, and with all of Soungoutou Magassa, Edson Alvarez, Mohamadou Kante, Tomas Soucek, Divine Mukasa and Lewis Orford on the books, there is not a burning need for another player in this position.

Peck will want to go somewhere he is a regular starter, just like he is at Sheffield United, and that may not be the case with the Hammers.

Furthermore, given his commitment to SUFC, it would be a surprise to see him leave mid-season for a club at the same level. If he is to be prised away from the Blades, it seems more likely that Peck heads for the Premier League.

#2187. Roberto Boninsegna, nato interista. Protagonista del Mondiale 1970 per sbaglio

Chissà se Spalletti non farebbe carte false per avere Roberto Boninsegna, in arte Bonimba grazie a Gianni Brera, nella sua squadra. Centravanti fisico, potente, mancino, coraggioso, dotato di grande personalità. Quello che non sono né Woltemade, né Kolo Muani.

Cresce nel settore giovanile dell'Inter, arrivando fino alle porte della prima squadra, prima di essere scartato da Helenio Herrera. Nel suo girovagare finisce a Cagliari, in coppia con Gigi Riva: condividono la camera, mentre a Skopje, durante un'invasione di campo, sia lui che Riva rimangono fuori dagli spogliatoi ad affrontare i tifosi avversari, con il compagno Nenè che entrato entrato e chiuso la porta a chiave. Si guadagna il Mondiale messicano del 1970, anche se per infortunio di Anastasi: uno a zero contro la Germania Ovest, assist a Gianni Rivera per il 4-3, gol dell'uno a uno in finale.

Rientra in nerazzurro dalla porta principale, diventando protagonista della lattina di Moenchengladbach, con la UEFA che decide di ripetere l'incontro: finisce 0-0 e il 4-2 dell'andata permette all'Inter di qualificarsi. Nel 1976 invece Bonimba finisce alla Juve, in cambio proprio di Anastasi, per scelta di Fraizzoli. Due Scudetti, una Coppa UEFA e una Coppa Italia per chi, alla Juventus, non ci voleva nemmeno andare. Oggi Roberto Boninsegna compie 83 anni.

#2184. West Ham backed to ‘come good’ by ex-EFL man Jobi McAnuff after Scott Parker chat

West Ham were relegated from the Premier League last season after they finished in the bottom three along with Burnley and Wolves. WHUFC are now eyeing an immediate promotion back from the Championship.

The Hammers are positioned in 9th place in the table after their first 15 fixtures and they are one point off the play-offs behind both Southampton and QPR following their 2-1 loss away at Blackburn Rovers last time out. They are back in action after the international break with a home clash against Preston North End at the London Stadium.

Former Watford, Cardiff City and Reading man McAnuff has delivered his verdict on the London outfit on BBC 72+ Podcast: “I think West Ham will come good still. I think that is a real good appointment, Scotty going in.”

He added: “I had a chat with him recently. Sometimes during these interviews there’s a line or two that just really stuck. He said he needed a bit of oxygen. He needed to get out of Burnley. You know, it was so tough. Tough ending to his tenure there.”

West Ham could do with a new goalkeeper in the upcoming January transfer window. Mads Hermansen has struggled to make an impact in this campaign, whilst Alphonse Areola has slipped down the pecking order at WHUFC.

The Hammers have conceded way too many goals so far this term. They need to tighten things up at the back if they are to compete for the top two.

They are doing fine at the top end of the pitch though and have no problems scoring goals. In fact, they are the top scorers in the division having found the net on a whopping 37 occasions already.

West Ham managed to keep hold of skipper Jarrod Bowen in the summer which was a massive boost. They then boosted their attacking departments with additions such as Manor Solomon and Joel Piroe.

West Ham’s appointment of Parker made perfect sense. He knows WHUFC already and understands what it takes to get out of the second tier having guided Fulham, AFC Bournemouth and Burnley up in the air.

As McAnuff has alluded to, the Hammers should come good over the coming weeks and months. They have a lot of quality at their disposal in attack and if they can stop leaking goals, there is no reason why they can’t challenge the likes of Birmingham City, Middlesbrough and Stoke City at the top.

Parker is an experienced head coach and needs to get some results now to get the fans fully on side. His side are winless in their last three matches, two of which were under his guidance.

West Ham’s next match against Preston North End is an ideal chance to return to winning ways. The Lilywhites are in the drop zone with Burnley and Derby County.

#2183. Nazionale, l'Italia conquista San Siro: rimonta da urlo contro la Francia

L'Italia di Roberto Mancini è uscita trionfante dallo scontro con la Francia, nonostante l'iniziale 0-1

Una serata da incorniciare per la Nazionale di Roberto Mancini, che davanti a un San Siro caldissimo e gremito da oltre 70.000 spettatori ha ribaltato la Francia con uno spettacolare 3-1. Gli Azzurri hanno riscattato subito il brivido iniziale e conquistato tre punti pesantissimi in questa sfida di Nations League.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

La gara si era messa subito in salita per la squadra di Mancini, tanto che al 10' minuto un errore in disimpegno ha permesso a Olise di sbloccare il risultato. Tuttavia, l'Italia non si è scomposta, ha alzato il baricentro e trovato il pareggio al 26' grazie a Pio Esposito su assist di Cambiaghi.

Nel secondo tempo gli Azzurri hanno cambiato marcia. Dopo un rigore sfortunato, al 62' ci ha pensato Davide Frattesi a far esplodere lo stadio con un inserimento fulmineo finalizzando al meglio un cross perfetto di Dimarco. La Francia ha accusato il colpo e, al 75', Pio Esposito ha trasformato con freddezza un calcio di rigore, firmando la sua splendida doppietta personale e blindando il definitivo 3-1.

#2181. Andoni Iraola: Liverpool cannot ‘sustain the level’ I want

Andoni Iraola has conceded that Liverpool cannot currently “sustain the level” he expects after his side squandered a two-goal lead for the second match in a row.

In the Spaniard’s first game at Anfield since becoming manager, Monaco fought back to win 3-2 by virtue of Paris Brunner’s late glancing header seven days on from Leeds United’s 4-2 victory in Chicago, emphasising Liverpool’s alarming lack of depth, particularly in defence.

“We played a good first half, especially a good 30 minutes. Right now we probably don’t have much more, in terms of the levels required to play like this. They deserved to turn around the game.

“We won the first two pre-season games, but the last two we cannot sustain the level we want for all of the game. As soon as we made some changes, players start to get tired and we cannot sustain that level. So, we have work to do.”

Alex on target 🎯 pic.twitter.com/PT6AYrLxyz

Alisson, Virgil van Dijk and Cody Gakpo all made their first appearances and contributed to a lively first-half in which Iraola’s commitment to man-to-man marking and an intensive pressing structure were evident out of possession. With the ball in the first half they demonstrated the kind of slick passing that Bournemouth employed to carve opponents open on the counter-attack.

A nimble one-two between Ryan Gravenberch and Dominik Szoboszlai allowed the Dutch midfielder to set up Alexander Isak to score with a shot from 16 yards and the most expensive player in English football history laid claim to the second, too, when he seemed to hook in a rebound from Lukas Hradecky’s save while lying in the six-yard box.

Later he would graciously acknowledge that he and Florian Wirtz had connected with the ball simultaneously and he was happy to yield the credit to his team-mate.

Our second goal of the game, scored by Wirtz… we think 🤔 pic.twitter.com/FUzA363Oxw

At that point it seemed that victory would be a cakewalk but just before half-time Aleksandr Golovin caught Van Dijk out with a feint and late swerve to the right, tripping over the Liverpool captain’s clumsy hack. Despite a heavy limp, Golovin hammered the penalty past Alisson’s dive to make it 2-1 at the break.

Iraola rationed his early changes at the start of the second half to two but Liverpool could not regain their rhythm and Mika Biereth capitalised on a Gravenberch error to score the equaliser after battling to be first to the rebound when Giorgi Mamardashvili saved his first effort, stroking his effort through Wataru Endo’s legs.

Cue five substitutions as soon as that goal had gone in and a raft more followed, killing the remaining half-hour of all momentum and cohesion. By the time Brunner exploited a dozy mix-up between Milos Kerkez and Federico Chiesa about whose responsibility he was at a corner to flash his header past Mamardashvili, only two of the 22 starters were still on the pitch.

Liverpool: Alisson (Mamardashvili 46), Tsimikas (Kerkez 59), Van Dijk (c) (Chambers 59), Ndukwe (Endo 46), Frimpong (Ramsay 75), Gravenberch (Nyoni 59), Gakpo (Ngumoha 59), Wirtz (McConnell 59), Koumas (Chiesa 75), Isak (Muñoz 59)Substitutes: Morrison, Davies.Goals: Isak (16), Wirtz (29)Monaco: Hradecky, Nazinho (Mawissa 75), Dier (c) (Kehrer 75), S Sané, Vanderson (Teze 46), Golovin (Idumbo 75), Cabral (Soubeir 75), Camara (Benzahra 64), Detourbet (Coulibaly 64), Biereth (Brunner 80), Abline (Konaté 64).Substitutes: Assab, Kohn, Lienard, Nibombe, Touré.  Goals: Golovin (44 pen), Biereth (56), Brunner (88)

They played well in the first half, as they did against Leeds, but lost all shape and structure in the second half to lose 3-2.

Yellow card for Konate for clattering into Endo late. That’s the last of the action.

Liverpool 2 Monaco 3 (Brunner)  Brilliant near-post glancing header from a devilishly whippy, dipping corner. Kerkez doing his best Rip van Winkle impression.

Diligent defensive work by Ngumoha stops Konate’s run down the outside left.

A final substitution, hopefully. Brunner replaces Biereth. Konate’s shot, a pearoller, is easily saved by Mamardashvili after A mix-up between Kerkez and Munoz.

As usual, the substitutions have killed the game. Watched Leeds vs RB Leipzig yesterday. They played two 60 minute matches which kept it more competitive for longer but still descended into a kickabout late on.

Mawissa, Kehrer, Idumbo and Soubeir have replaced Nazinho, Dier, Golovin and Cabral.

Monaco also made changes, four I think. Will catch up with then once they’re confirmed. Ramsey is into the game straight away tp stop Coulibaly’s shot.

Ramsay, Chiesa and Elliott are coming on for Szoboszlai, Frimpong and Koumas. Lovely applause for Elliott.

Konate sweeps a left-foot shot from the left of the box past the right post with Mamardashvili forced into a headlong dive. Liverpool have lost their defensive shape and intensity, allowing Monaco to punch holes through the lines with some direct running.

Nazinho gets round Frimpong down the Monaco left and cuts back the perfect pass to put it on a plate for Coulibaly who hammers a left-foot shot from the right of the D that Mamardashvili throws up his hands to block. That would have stung. Good save. A flag would have meant it wouldn’t have counted but even so Frimpong’s defensive work wasn’t good enough.

It’s been very scrappy since the changes but then Ngumoha pounces on to a loose ball, glides up the right and tries to play Koumas in behind the back four. Sadly, he overhits the pass which trickles out for a goal-kick. Cue an ovation and cries of ‘Rio! Rio!’

Three changes for Monaco with Detoubet, Abline and Camara departing for Benzhara, Coulibaly and Konaté.

Ngumoha has gone to the right wing and Koumas is playing through the middle as a false nine.

Liverpool make five changes with Gakpo, Van Dijk, Wirtz, Tsimikas and Isak departing while Munoz makes his ‘debut’ with Ngumoha, Nyoni, Chambers, McConell and Kerkez.

Liverpool 2 Monaco 2 (Biereth) Mistake by Gravenberch allows Golovin to play a pass into the left half of the box for Biereth. His first shot is well saved by Mamardashvili at full stretch but his tenacity allowed him to get to the rebound ahead of Endo, swivel and stick his second effort in to equalise.

Monaco overplay at the edge of the box, Detourbet, Biereth and Abline playing ‘after you. Claud’, instead of shooting.

A slick, short passing move between Gravenberch, Wirtz and Isak ends with Isak on the deck in the box after Gravenberch’s step-over allowed the ball to run to him. Isak took a touch to move the ball on to his left but gave the defender a glimmer of it and he hacked it away before felling Isak as well as collateral damage.

Quiet start to the half. Biereth just pressed Mamardashvili but the keeper managed to sweep the ball away even though under more pressure than he would have expected.

Liverpool try to play out from the back with Mamardashvili spearing a straight pass to Gravenberch who tried to knock it round the corner for Endo but the ball skidded under his studs and out for a throw-in.

Isak is apparently claiming both goals. Replays are indistinct. He could have clipped it in off his heel as he had a swing at the ball while grounded at the same time as Wirtz.

Our second goal of the game, scored by Wirtz… we think 🤔 pic.twitter.com/FUzA363Oxw

Endo has slotted in at centre-back alongside Van Dijk.

Half-time changes: Mamardashvili for Alisson and Endo for Ndukwe.

For Monaco, Teze for Vanderson at right-back.

Much to admire in that first-half performance from Liverpool’s front six and centre-backs, the latter two particularly out of possession and winning it back. The man-to-man system is working well for them. But they should be out of sight by now instead of leading by only a goal.

Liverpool free-kick on the right after a foul on Koumas. Szoboszlai whips in the cross and Van Dijk gets up first to the left of the penalty spot but bludgeons his header about a foot wide of the right post.

Charlie Adam says the penalty was ‘a soft one’ but Van Dijk did not adjust when Golovin shifted it to his right, stuck out a leg and Golovim understandably decided to topple over the hurdle.

Liverpool 2 Monaco 1 (Golovin, pen) Speared it into the top right. Alisson went the right way but was beaten for pace and power.

The referee, Jarred Gillett, gives Monaco a penalty. It was deserved, too. Golovin went on a driving run down the middle, shifted it to his right and was tripped by Van Dijk.

Liverpool are well worth their lead. They are streets ahead of Monaco.

A microcosm of the game so far: Liverpool pass goes astray, a player wins it back – this time Gakpo – by snapping into a tackle before laying it off to his left for the unmarked Tsimikas to skim a low shot about a foot wide of the left post from 25 yards.

Monaco have done very little since that wee salvo in the opening minute but just now Abline was given too much space from distance but whacked his shot into the Kop.

Big cheer from the crowd for Hugo Ekitike

The impressive Koumas diddles Nazinho with a Cruyff turn but then thumps his shot straight into Hradecky’s chest.

Liverpool 2 Monaco 0 (Wirtz) Pass chipped out to Koumas down the right who arcs a cross to the back post. Gakpo meets it with a header back across goal to Isak whose first effort from a couple of yards is blocked but the ball gets stuck under Isak’s falling body and Wirtz is Johnny on the Spot to stab it in from a 18 inches as Dier tried to scramble it away.

Liverpool have been impressive so far but if there has been a weakness it’s still the balance in central midfield defensively. Going forward both Gravenberch and Szobiszlai have dovetailed together well. They win the ball back when Van Dijk steps out and the midfield two fly forward to tee up Koumas for a shot that strikes Dier.

Chance for Wirtz, who opens his body from the left of the D to try to bend a shot into the bottom right corner but fizzes it the wrong side of the post.

It’s been pretty breathless since the goal, Liverpool dozily letting Monaco through the middle before twice cutting Monaco apart in midfield only for Isak to overrun a pass in the box delivered by Wirtz and then Koumas to shoot over.

Liverpool 1 Monaco 0 (Isak) Chip to Gakpo down the left of the box, he clips it first time back to Gravenberch in the D who lays it off to Szoboszlai. Ping-ping-ping. Excellent move from Liverpool, instigated by Ndukwe’s fine tackle on Biereth. He knocks it short to Gravenberch who arrows a 60-yard pass out to Koumas hurtling down the right. He measures his cross perfectly for Gakpo stealing in off the left wing and though he connects powerfully with the meat of his brow, he bashes it straight at the keeper. He was offside in any case, it transpires.

Charlie Adam on co-comms points out that Liverpool are going man-to-man now in classic Iraola/Bielsa style and when the camera goes high you can see it, especially with the full-backs.

Liverpool are carving the middle of the park up with incisive passing. Szoboszlai starts a move that sends Isak down the left of the box. He traps the chip, turns back to face away from goal and taps a pass back to Wirtz who gallops on to it and bends a shot that Hradecky dives low to his left to beat away with a firm left wrist. Frimpong pounces on the rebound and clips a tame effort straight at Hradecky who had quickly restored himself to the vertical.

Van Dijk gobbles up a long pass over Ndukwe, snuffing out any possibility of Biereth getting there first.

Tsimikas is free down the left but overhits his cross on the run when Isak was begging for a cut-back. The left-back went for the back post and ballooned his centre behind, miles beyond Koumas and Wirtz.

Liverpool defend the corner solidly and Frimpong races away on the counter, bombing on to the ball and sprinting into the Monaco half. But his touch is not as sound as his pace and Monaco deal with him when he fluffs his control.

A period of applause in memory of Kevin Keegan ❤️ pic.twitter.com/AkPZG7babe

Liverpool kick off defending the Kop. They roll the ball back to Frimpong who chips a long pass up the right to Koumas who hares on to the pass down the right and crosses, just beyond Isak. Hradecky gathers and bowls the ball out. A mistake by Tisimikas lets Biereth barrel through the inside-right to hammer a shot that Alisson turns behind for a corner.

The crowd applauds in the modern way. My goodness he was a terrific player. Saw him play for Liverpool, Southampton and Newcastle. Absolutely relentless.

Virgil van Dijk has the pennant. Liverpool are wearing all red with the paint splashes of the 1990 kit. Monaco are in a very pale green.

For me it’s a special game, most of the players and people will be ‘it’s [just] a pre-season friendly’, but for me it’s a lot more than this. It will be the first time I have the chance to go out, to feel this stadium and I hope we can also perform well.

It’s a good game for us, because it’s going to be demanding, it’s a team that wants to dominate and they also want to press high so we will see how we are.

I think the cases of Ali[sson], Cody, and Virgil I think it’s their first game. It shouldn’t go longer than 45 minutes, maybe a little bit more if they feel comfortable.

Also for some of the others Ryan, Flo, and Alex, it’s the second game so we have to be aware of those things.

But the league starts in two weeks, so we cannot wait also too much; we have to give them quality minutes.

I think today is going to be a good reference, two games – Monaco and Como – teams that try dominate the game, they are very good in possession, so they will demand us the best without the ball. If we want to control the game and also physical teams, they are probably a very good test.

I hope we are at the level and we can do very good games.

Alisson, Van Dijk and Gakpo return, Ndukwe continues in defence.

The Reds to take on AS Monaco at Anfield!Watch the match LIVE on All Red Video 📺

Good afternoon and welcome to live coverage of the first of two Liverpool home friendlies after their preseason tour of the United States. Andoni Iraola’s side saw off Wrexham and Sunderland in their first two matches but gave away a two-goal lead at half-time in the final fixture and ended up being beaten 4-2 by Leeds United in Chicago in the last game after multiple changes. What did we learn? Not a huge amount in the absence of Virgil van Dijk, Alisson, Víctor Muñoz, Cody Gakpo and Alexis Mac Allister, the first four of whom are available today other than an ongoing vulnerability from set-pieces and Curtis Jones looking like he is in desperate need of a fresh start.

This afternoon at Anfield they take on AS Monaco who finished seventh in Ligue 1 this season but are under new management with the recruitment of the former Chelsea and Atlético left-back Filipe Luís as head coach. It’s quite the coup for Le Rocher given he led Flamengo to their first Brazilian title in five years, the Copa Libertadores and the Copa do Brasil in his 18 months in charge.

In pre-season so far he has overseen a 5-2 win over fourth-tier AS Saint-Priest, a 2-0 defeat by Sporting Clube, a 2-2 draw with Cercle Bruges and a 1-0 victory over Getafe. They have sold their best player, Maghnes Akliouche, to PSG for £43m and Aladji Bamba to Newcastle for £34m but have so far retained Mamadou Coulibaly and Folarin Balogun, signed and made Ansu Fati’s loan move into a long-term deal.

Iraola may well persevere with Trey Nyoni in midfield who, after his performances on tour, ought to be on the threshold of a breakthrough season and it would be interesting to see him paired with Ryan Gravenberch just behind Dominik Szoboszlai.

Ifeanyi Ndukwe, who played well in America after coming on for the injured Joe Gomez, does not have a UK work permit and will have to go abroad on loan for the season but Jérémy Jacquet, who has yet to feature, was due to have a fitness test this morning and would be a welcome to boost to a squad whose defence still requires significant reinforcements.

#2180. Napoli, l’ass.Cosenza: “Stadio Maradona? Alte probabilità per Euro 2032. Il cantiere aprirà nel 2028”

A Radio Napoli Centrale, nel corso di Un Calcio alla Radio è intervenuto l’assessore alle infrastrutture Edoardo Cosenza: “L’allarme bomba inviato alle scuole italiane? Difficile capire se l’email inviate agli istituti siano effettive. Però per principi di sicurezza, capisco che si sia reagito in questo modo. Non sono scherzi da ragazzi. Bisogna capire cosa ci sia dietro. Napoli città ospitante per EURO 2032? La data limite di fine ottobre per scoprire se la città avrebbe ospitato gli Europei era stata data dall’UEFA, ma da parecchio tempo avevo intuito che si sarebbe andato oltre. Di Napoli parlano benissimo, come parlano benissimo di altre città. Gli incontri con UEFA e Abodi sono andati benissimo. La probabilità che la città ospiti gli Europei è alta. Le grandi città come Milano, Roma e Napoli hanno un forte vantaggio competitivo. Così come Torino. Ci sono fibrillazioni in altre città come Milano per l’abbattimento di San Siro, ma qui non ci sono problemi. Sono due anni che stiamo lavorando per questo. Quando un’opera supera i 50 milioni di euro, deve avere l’ok del Consiglio Superiore dei lavori pubblici. Questo non comporta l’allungamento dei tempi, la procedura si risolverà entro fine gennaio. La delibera Cipess dovrà essere deliberata dalla Corte dei Conti. A quel punto, il nostro progetto sarà ancora più affidabile. Sul terzo anello dello stadio Maradona si va avanti, ma anche per la riqualificazione dei parcheggi. Quando lo stadio Maradona diventerà un cantiere? Se la gara la faremo partire nel primo trimestre del 2027, con conclusione attorno alla metà del 2027, allora poi servirà il progetto esecutivo. Dall’inizio del 2028 saremo pronti per l’inizio dei lavori. Il nostro primo obiettivo è mettere a posto il terzo anello, per non ridurre la capienza dello stadio. Quando faremo i lavori per il primo anello, garantiremo la possibilità di andare al terzo anello. Non si andrà mai meno dei 54 mila posti attuali. Le prime prove le avremo nel breve. In funzione dei lavori al primo anello, apriremo poi il terzo anello. Il terzo anello lo apriremo man mano che ci servirà, ma i lavori inizieranno certamente prima dei lavori al primo anello dello stadio Maradona”.

#2179. Player who left Sheffield Wednesday in the summer in line for new EFL contract

Sheffield Wednesday cut ties with the centre-back at the end of last season following their relegation from down to League One. SWFC decided not to hand him a fresh contract and he subsequently became a free agent in late June.

Iorfa, 31, weighed up his options over the summer as he searched for the right opportunity. He then eventually penned short-term terms with Rotherham United in September running until January after opting to stick around in South Yorkshire.

In this latest update regarding his long-term future with the Millers, McClaren has declared their intention to keep hold of him and has said, as per a report by Rotherham Advertiser: “He’s performing well for us and he’s a good leader. The lads like him, I like him. He’s got energy and personality. We’re conversing with him every day. We’d like to extend it. Nearer the time, I’m sure we’ll address it.”

Rotherham United went down to League Two earlier this year and have since struggled to adapt to life in the fourth tier. They are only above the drop zone on goal difference ahead of Colchester United.

Iorfa was a good servant for Sheffield Wednesday during his time with the Owls. He spent seven years at SWFC and made 222 appearances in all competitions, chipping in with seven goals from the back.

Despite Rotherham United’s struggles on the pitch over recent times, he has enjoyed plenty of game time with the Millers. He has made nine outings since his switch, eight of which have come in the league.

Iorfa was snapped up by Alex Bruce’s side to boost their defensive department. He gives them more competition and depth in his position.

The ex-England youth international spent the last three years in the Championship before his transfer to the New York Stadium. Therefore, the ex-Wolves man was seen as a bit of a coup for Rotherham United at the time.

Handing an extension to Iorfa is a no-brainer for Rotherham United. He is a vastly experienced operator in the EFL and will be a useful character to have in and around the dressing room.

The Millers need to rise away from the bottom two soon though. A club of their size should not be battling to avoid slipping into non-league.

Bruce was appointed as their head coach in the summer but he has won only two league games. He was previously Karl Robinson’s number two at Salford City and has also managed Macclesfield in the past.

Rotherham United won 2-0 away at Bromley in the FA Cup first round last time out. Next up is a trip to Blundell Park to take on Grimsby Town after the international break.

#2178. Un talento al giorno, Ignacio Ovando: Scaloni lo stima, Baccin... anche

L'Inter segue da vicino Ignacio Ovando, nato il 29 giugno 2007 a Gualeguaychú: parliamo di un difensore centrale argentino del Rosario Central alto 1,82 m. Cresciuto nelle giovanili del club (dopo un inizio al Central Córdoba), è stato arretrato da attaccante a centrale e ha firmato il primo contratto professionistico nel 2025. Ha esordito in Primera División il 5 ottobre 2025 nella vittoria sul River Plate e si è rapidamente imposto come titolare, contribuendo al titolo argentino 2025. Nel 2026 è diventato pilastro della squadra anche in Copa Libertadores, totalizzando oltre 40 presenze con diversi gol e assist.

Tecnicamente è un centrale moderno di piede destro, abile con i piedi, solido nel gioco aereo e con ottima lettura delle situazioni. Si distingue per personalità, posizionamento e capacità di impostare dal basso. Convocato da Scaloni per allenarsi con la Nazionale maggiore nell’estate 2026, è tra i prospetti più seguiti del calcio argentino e figura nel mirino di Inter e Napoli, con il Rosario Central che valuta il cartellino intorno ai 15 milioni di dollari.

#2177. Italia, Frattesi: "Obiettivo? Togliermi le etichette. Su Gattuso e Mancini..."

Davide Frattesi ha rilasciato delle dichiarazioni ai microfoni di Rai Sport poco dopo la fine dell'incontro tra Italia e Francia, terminato 3-1 per gli Azzurri

Davide Frattesi ha rilasciato delle dichiarazioni ai microfoni di Rai Sport poco dopo la fine dell'incontro tra Italia e Francia, terminato 3-1 per gli Azzurri.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Una serata emozionante, forse la doppietta con l’Ucraina serviva per un altro scopo però far gol qui è sempre speciale perché in quella porta c’è stato il gol più importante della mia carriera e sono contento di aver segnato questa sera, in questa notte bellissima.

Lazio e Nazionale? Sono due situazioni analoghe, nella Lazio, nonostante la prima sconfitta, siamo riusciti a compattarci e a trovare entusiasmo, credo che quella sia la cosa più importante anche qui grazie al mister e ai ragazzi nuovi che hanno portato una ventata d’aria fresca. Quando c’è un ambiente sano è tutto più facile. Io più che per la vittoria sono contento per il carattere e l’abnegazione che c’è stata.

Con Gattuso abbiamo qualche discussione perché mi chiede di esaltare le caratteristiche che ho, io invece vorrei fare al 100% quello che non so fare. Però quello è sempre stato il mio obiettivo, levarmi le etichette che non rispecchiano quello che sono davvero. Sono contento di aver fiducia da Gattuso e da Mancini.

Quando riesco ad avere quei 10-15 metri e riesco ad accelerare in corsa riesco a coprire una porzione di campo più grande. Come oggi che sono arrivato dietro al difensore, mi sono staccato e ho fatto gol. Per me è importante arrivare da dietro in corsa perché così io posso decidere come attaccare.

Gol contro il Napoli? Quello è merito di Gattuso che ci massacra durante la settimana, la forma fisica c'è.

Mancini? Dopp il gol stavamo parlando della fase difensiva.

#2175. ‘A club I like’: Andy Woodman quizzed on Crawley Town managerial post as search for new boss continues

Andy Woodman was sacked by Bromley last month following a 4-0 defeat to Leicester City at home. The dismissal came as a surprise for many at Hayes Lane and beyond, given the remarkable job Woodman had done in his five years in charge.

Crawley Town are currently without a manager after they sacked Colin Kazim-Richards in October. And, Woodman has been quizzed on the vacancy and whether it is one he would be interested in.

Speaking on BBC Sport’s 72+ EFL podcast, Woodman said: “Crawley’s a club that is very close to me. My son [Freddie Woodman] went on loan. I’ve grown up around the area. It’s a football club I’ve got a lot of time for.

“They have got some big games coming up against teams around them, and if they can climb out of the bottom, they can start getting that club back to where they should be. I do hope they do, because it’s a club I do like.”

Former Bromley manager Woodman will rightly feel hard done by when it comes to his sacking. With this, he will want to prove the decision-makers at the Ravens that they were incorrect to fire him.

Woodman could have the opportunity to return to management with Crawley Town at the bottom of the table, and a big task would await him if he did take the job. But, if the 55-year-old returns to the dugout and gets it right with the Red Devils, it would be another feather in his cap.

Crawley only narrowly survived last season, finishing in 22nd. Their slide from League One straight to the bottom end of League Two means the next boss has a big task on their hands.

Woodman’s time at Bromley was nothing short of incredible. He helped guide the Ravens to the Football League for the first time in their history, then went one step further in earning promotion to League One.

The 55-year-old was a big hit in just his second managerial role, and he would be backed to bring better fortunes to Broadfield Stadium if he was to be hired. If the Red Devils could convince Woodman to be their new manager, they can quickly start looking back up the League Two table.

League Two’s bottom side needs an injection of life, and Woodman has not written off a move to the club.

The big question is whether Wodman would be willing to drop down to League Two and take charge of the side sitting in a perilous position. His work with Bromley means he arguably should be aiming a little higher, but the connection between him and Crawley could sway him into taking charge of their rescue mission.

There is plenty of work to be done at Broadfield Stadium between now and the end of the season, but the former Bromley boss may see the challenge as one he wants to take on.

#2174. Lazio, il centravanti ombra di Gattuso e Mancini: Frattesi è già a sei gol in stagione

È tornato il numero 8. Quel centrocampista in grado di fare tutto, il classico "box to box" che nei primi anni Duemila è stato meravigliosamente incarnato da due fuoriclasse come Gerrard e Lampard. Nessun paragone tecnico, sia chiaro. I numeri però parlano e quello che sta facendo in questo inizio di stagione Davide Frattesi è un qualcosa di straordinario. Lui che per ritrovarsi dopo un anno difficile all'Inter ha scelto di farsi allenare da un ex numero 8, seppur con caratteristiche diverse, come Genanro Gattuso. La stagione che sta vivendo il centrocampista tra Lazio e nazionale è semplicemente straordinaria ed è stata ulteriormente impreziosita dalla sua prima da "ex" a San Siro. Il gol contro la Francia ha portato gli Azzurri in vantaggio regalando un successo che vale il primato nel girone di Nations League a 90 minuti dalla fine.

Che Davide Frattesi abbia i gol nelle gambe non lo si scopre sicuramente quest'anno. Prima della scorsa stagione infatti l'ex Inter e Sassuolo era reduce da sei anni consecutivi con almeno 4 reti realizzate tra Serie B e Serie A. Quota che ha già raggiunto in appena 11 partite con la maglia della Lazio, numeri che crescono ulteriormente se si considera anche la nazionale. Sei gol a metà novembre in proiezione possono portarlo ben oltre la doppia cifra, sfiorare addirittura i 15 gol stagionali che sarebbero numeri da attaccante, altro che centrocampista. E intanto la rete di ieri lo ha portato in un ristretto club della storia della nazionale italiana. Sono solo otto i centrocampisti ad aver segnato almeno 10 gol in nazionale, appena cinque dal dopoguerra e Frattesi è il migliore per media gol a partita con 0.26. In vetta alla classifica marcatori tra i centrocampisti c'è De Rossi con 21 reti in 117 presenze, mentre il secondo per media gol è Rivera con 0.23 grazie alle sue 14 reti in 60 presenze.

#2173. Arsenal lose second successive pre-season friendly after parading Guimaraes

Arsenal suffered back-to-back pre-season defeats as they lost 3-2 to Borussia Dortmund in the Emirates Cup on Sunday.

The loss to the German side followed a 3-1 defeat by Real Betis on Wednesday night in Dublin.

Before kick-off, Bruno Guimarães was paraded to the Arsenal faithful after completing his £75m move from Newcastle on Saturday.

But enthusiasm about the Brazilian’s arrival was dampened when Dortmund took the lead at the Emirates inside the opening 10 minutes. Felix Nmecha’s incisive pass into the penalty area was met by the run of Samuele Inácio, who made no mistake with a clinical finish into the far corner.

The Bundesliga side doubled their lead just before the half-hour mark as 18-year-old Greek international Konstantinos Karetsas curled one in from the edge of the box.

Ten minutes after half-time, Arsenal pulled a goal back through Ethan Nwaneri, who scored from Christos Tzolis’s cross. However, moments later Dortmund restored their two-goal advantage as Joane Gadou smashed home from a corner.

Arsenal kept their hopes alive through a Viktor Gyokeres penalty after Tzolis was brought down, but Dortmund were able to hold on to claim victory. After the full-time whistle, there was a penalty shoot-out, which Arsenal won 5-4.

Arsenal still have a number of players to return to action following their World Cup exploits, including Declan Rice, Bukayo Saka and Noni Madueke. England and Real Madrid midfielder Jude Bellingham was in attendance at the Emirates to watch his younger brother Jobe in action for Dortmund.

As well as Guimarães, there was another addition on display at the Emirates: Arsenal have added last year’s title win to the list of honours displayed inside the stadium.

Arsenal play Cesc Fàbregas’s Como on Wednesday, before taking on Manchester City in the Community Shield next weekend. Mikel Arteta’s side then start the defence of their Premier League crown at home against newly promoted Coventry City.

The former Chelsea man receives some boos as he steps up but calmly slots home to keep Dortmund’s hopes alive.

Meyer goes the right way but there is far too much power on this penalty from Martinelli. Meyer had no chance of saving that one.

We have the first penalty miss of this shootout as Meslier guesses the right way and saves what was a pretty poor penalty from Albert. Advantage Arsenal.

It is perhaps not a massive surprise but the game has lost some flow with all the changes both sides have made during this second half.

There is set to be a penalty shootout at the end of this one, no matter what the final result is.

Former Arsenal midfielder Cesc Fabregas is in attendance, with his Como side taking on Arsenal here on Wednesday.

With just under 15 minutes to go at the Emirates, Arsenal are going to make a number of changes. Plenty of youngsters are entering the fray as are a few experienced players like Jesus and Martinelli.

The Swede sends Meyer the wrong way and slots into the bottom right corner. It is now 3-2 and this game is not over with 20 minutes to go.

Tzolis goes down inside the Dortmund penalty area and referee Chris Kavanagh points to the spot. He was caught inside the box and there did not appear to be too many complaints from the visitors.

Nwaneri comes close to getting his second goal of the half. Tzolis rolls it across the face of the penalty area to Nwaneri, who hits it first time and forces Meyer into a good save to his right, pushing it onto the post.

In the aftermath of that Dortmund goal, there are a raft of changes for both sides. Arsenal have brought on Salmon and Hincapie for Calafiori and Gabriel.

Dortmund have brought on the experience of the likes of Sabitzer and Guirassy.

Arsenal fail to deal with a corner from the right and the ball ends up with Gadou at the back post. Gadou takes one touch before finding the net, despite the best efforts of numerous Arsenal defenders on the line.

Should Inacio have gone down? The young Italian is played in behind the Arsenal defence and has Calafiori all over his back. Inacio ends up losing possession on the edge of the Arsenal penalty area but had he gone down Calafiori could have been in trouble.

Mikel Arteta’s side reduce Dortmund’s lead to just the one goal. Tzolis cuts it back from the left side of the box and picks out Nwaneri eight yards out from goal in the centre of the box. Nwaneri makes no mistake, smashing home and Arsenal are back in this game.

That is good defending from Nwaneri. Just as Chukwuemeka was lining up a shot just outside the box, Nwaneri tracks back and steals it off Chukwuemeka.

White is found on the right side of the box but is denied by Meyer in the Dortmund goal. The subsequent corner comes to nothing.

Reports are that Jobe’s older brother Jude is at the Emirates today, not that Arsenal fans should think that Jude is swapping Real Madrid for the red half of north London.

Arsenal took the Premier League trophy with them to Dublin on Wednesday night and it is on show today at the Emirates. They kick off the defence of their crown on opening night against newly-promoted Coventry is just under two weeks.

Counting down until kick-off ⏰ Make sure you're in your seats by 1.45pm (UK), Gooners - we have a very special guest in store 👀

When Bruno Guimarães signed for Newcastle United from Lyon in January 2022, much was made of the idea that he had rejected Arsenal in favour of a move to St James’ Park. The Brazilian played along with it, posting a video on his social media pages which featured multiple newspaper headlines about Arsenal wanting to buy him.

The Newcastle fans, still delirious about the recent takeover by Saudi Arabia’s Public Investment Fund, understandably lapped it up. Their dream was to overthrow the established powers in English football, and the capture of Guimarães was regarded as a sign that they were truly ready to challenge teams of Arsenal’s stature and wealth.

Even the lyrics to the midfielder’s chant referred to the north Londoners, as Newcastle fans sang that Guimarães “could have signed for Arsenal” but chose instead to play “in the famous black and white”.

Yesterday Arsenal announced the signing of Brazilian midfielder Bruno Guimaraes from Newcastle for £75m on a four-year deal, with the option of a further year. Here is what the Brazilian had to say:

“I feel good, I feel amazing. I am glad for the opportunity. Since the first time I spoke to (Andrea) Berta and Mikel (Arteta) I was excited. I just felt inside of me that I need a new challenge in my life and I think to be an Arsenal player is going to be an exciting challenge in my life.

“I think after playing two World Cups, I am at the point of my life, I think I need a challenge like this. I want to win trophies, I want to make history and I think I am in the right place to do it. I am so excited to start. We have tough games to play this season so the squad is going to be very, very important and I am hopeful we can achieve our dreams.”

Arsenal: Kepa; White, Mosquera, Gabriel, Calafiori; Lewis-Skelly, Havertz, Odegaard; Dowman, Gyokeres, Tzolis.Substitutes: Meslier, Setford, Hincapie, Jesus, Martinielli, Vieira, Nwaneri, Nelson, Ibrahim, Kabia, Ogunnaike, Julienne, O’Neill, Salmon.

Borussia Dortmund: Meyer; Svensson, Inacio, Bensebaini, Gadou; Bellingham, Nmecha, Mane; Karetsas, Beier, Silva.Substitutes: Drewes, Guirassy, Chukwuemeka, Sabitzer, Lerema, Prates, Albert, Kabar, Yamamoto, Reggiani, Riedl.

INS:Illan Meslier from Leeds, FreeChristos Tzolis from Club Brugge, £34mBruno Guimaraes from Newcastle, £75m

Piero Hincapie’s move from Bayer Leverkusen was made permanent this summer.

Checking in for the Emirates Cup 🛬 pic.twitter.com/mQ1eAESKHY

Premier League champions Arsenal continue their preparations for the new season as they take on Borussia Dortmund in the Emirates Cup. Arsenal beat Spanish side Athletic Bilbao 3-0 in the 2025 edition, with goals from Viktor Gyokeres, Bukayo Saka and Kai Havertz sealing victory. These sides met during the 2014/15 Champions League group stages, with both games being won 2-0 by the home side.

Arsenal beat Girona 4-1 just over a week ago but fell to a 3-1 defeat to Real Betis in Dublin on Wednesday night. Reflecting on that defeat, Mikel Arteta expressed his frustration at the way his side conceded the goals.

“I am disappointed with the result,” Arteta told Arsenal’s in-house media. “We are here to win football matches. We had some fantastic moments in the first half, the way we played, the way we opened them up. We have to score first, clearly, in the two chances that we had before the score.

“I am very disappointed in the manner that we conceded the first goal, the two giveaways leading to the other two goals. It is true that the finishing quality from them was exceptional, but to win a football match, you have to compete at a different level, especially in those areas of the pitch and that is the learning from today [last Wednesday].”

This is in fact Arsenal’s first game at the Emirates since being crowned Premier League champions, as they sealed the title with a game to spare, with that final match of last season being away at Crystal Palace. After today, Arsenal have two more games, including the Community Shield in a week’s time, before they kick off the defence of their Premier League crown at home against newly-promoted Coventry. The likes of Declan Rice, Bukayo Saka and Martin Zubimendi are still yet to return after their World Cup exploits.

Dortmund, led by Niko Kovac, finished second in the Bundesliga last season, 16 points off winners Bayern Munich. They have played four pre-season friendlies thus far, winning twice and losing twice. The likes of Karim Adeyemi, Julian Brandt and Niklas Sule have left the club this summer.

#2172. Harry Kane sends message to England fans and media amid hype over Middlesbrough star Will Lankshear

Middlesbrough have seen Will Lankshear emerge as a new star since his summer arrival. The 20-year-old’s record of 15 goals in 16 games has brought great excitement, with England boss Thomas Tuchel monitoring his performances and pundits touting him as a future replacement for Harry Kane.

However, the man Lankshear is drawing comparisons to has urged patience over his development. As quoted by Sky Sports, Ballon d’Or winner and England star Kane said on the Boro ace: “We talk about a striker having that goalscoring instinct and I think he’s one of those players for sure.

“He’s still in his early development. What he’s doing right now is perfect for him, playing loads of games, scoring goals, getting confidence. Whether he comes into the senior side it’s down to Thomas and if he did, I’m sure he would learn loads here surrounded by the attackers we have.

“It’s not a time to put too much pressure on him. I know there’s a lot of talk about who will replace me, but just let him do what he’s doing. It’s great for his development, he’s doing extremely well.”

Lankshear has long been viewed as a striker to keep an eye on in the years to come. He spent time in the Arsenal academy and only spent a year at Sheffield United before Spurs paid to bring him back down to North London.

His promising displays on loan at Oxford United meant there was some frustration among Spurs fans over the decision to sell him. Lankshear has quickly come good on a sizeable £20m total deal at Middlesbrough, and naturally, 15 goals in 16 games has brought excitement among England fans and in the wider media.

Based on what we’ve seen so far, there’s no reason to believe that Lankshear won’t reach a high level in the game. However, aged 21 and with 80 senior appearances to his name, the Boro man is very much in the formative years of his career.

From an international perspective, while Kane is still playing and firing in the goals, there is no drastic urgency to rush the next star striker through the setup. Lankshear and the coaches around him at club and international level can afford to take their time, allowing him to stay focused on his game and keep growing on and off the pitch.

Both publicly and privately, Lankshear will have people around him telling him to stay focused on his game amid growing noise around his name. However, there is perhaps no one better to say this than Kane – the man he is being compared to and touted to replace for England.

The 33-year-old wasn’t an overnight success himself, spending time out on loan at Leyton Orient, Millwall, Norwich City and Leicester City before breaking into the Spurs first-team shortly before he turned 21.

It took time for Kane to reach his lofty heights and become the star we know today, so there is no need to rush Lankshear. He’s thriving with Middlesbrough and gaining both experience and confidence, which will be key if he is to live up to his exciting potential.
